Each of the 32 T1 objection rows (AV-07 4 · AV-01 7 · AV-04 7 · AV-02 8 · AV-03 6) carries the component set its OWN avatar's analyst named when marking it dissolved (YES) in that partial's domino table; rows marked NO (money, proof/results, legitimacy, hidden cost, refund terms, income/timeline — "a belief cannot create funds / a mechanism cannot supply a number") stay undissolved under every candidate. A candidate dissolves a row when it carries that row's whole component set. [D] analyst read inherited from the partials; nothing re-judged.

| OBJ-03 is this company legitimate / scam | T1 | NO | the mechanism does not prove the seller is real; the domain scores low on two trust tools (PT-16, F-14/F-15) |
| OBJ-01 money now | T1 | NO | a smaller bet is still a bet when funds are cancelled or capped (Q-O-0051, Q-O-0057) |
| OBJ-07 how much will it make | T1 | NO | the domino is a process belief; income cannot be promised (PT-09) and must not become the domino |
| OBJ-05 spouse / joint funds | T2 | NO | the decider is not on the ad (Q-O-0134) |
| OBJ-42.AV-04 Etsy suspends my account | T2 | NO | platform risk sits outside the service; not guaranteed (PT-05 risk-disclaimer lists account approval/longevity) |

`BD-1: T1 dissolved 1/7 (OBJ-11), T2 dissolved 3/5, verdict DOMINO: PARTIAL`
- **Surviving T1:** OBJ-04, OBJ-41.AV-04, OBJ-02, OBJ-03, OBJ-01, OBJ-07.
- **Alternative framing (one sentence):** "A store that proves itself in small, capped tests you approve — in your name, with every cost and refund condition on paper before you pay — is the key to an income that isn't hostage to your job."
- **mechanism_note → Step 10:** the T1 survivors are TERMS and TRUST (OBJ-02, OBJ-03, OBJ-41.AV-04, OBJ-04), which neither LOCK-A nor LOCK-B carries; AV-04's sceptic is procedural (09: "contracts, refund windows"). The unlocked MK-07 "Borrowed Keys" trust bridge / ROUTE 5 "Keys In Your Name" and a published full-cost + refund-conditions sheet are the nearest cover. OBJ-01 and OBJ-07 are money/income objections no mechanism should dissolve (PT-09).

### §2.AV-02 — Paid-and-Got-Nothing (carried from 11-partials/11-BELIEFS.AV-02.md §2)

#### §2 · BIG DOMINO — BD-1.AV-02 (this avatar's candidate; Step 12 reconciles one per brand)  ⟨AV-02⟩

- **bd_statement:** "A store opened in your own name that a team keeps working on after launch day is the key to an income that isn't hostage to a job."
- **md_id:** MD-01 · **lf8_root:** #3 Freedom from fear, pain & danger (inside MD-01's routes #1/#3/#5/#8; AV-02's primary LF8 is #3 "of being robbed again", 09) · **desire_wording:** "I want to have an income that is my own" (08 MD-01 T1) · **mechanism_ref:** M-2 = LOCK-B UMS (team stays after go-live: early review, corrections, rollout — PT-04, PT-08) + the MK-07 ownership component (F-11) · M-1 = LOCK-B UMP (build sold as the finish line; builder leaves at launch) + MK-07 (store held in the builder's account until transfer).
- Desire anchoring test: PASS — names MD-01 with an lf8_root; not a functional outcome (the store is the vehicle, the desire is the income that is her own).

**Domino test over every OBJ-## (06 OBJ-01..11 + AV-02's own OBJ-12..15.AV-02, §8).** "If she fully believed BD-1, is the objection irrelevant?" [D] analyst read, one line each.

| OBJ-## | tier | dissolved? | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| OBJ-01 money / not the right time | T1 | NO | a belief cannot create funds; honest answer is qualification (HONEST-BOX), never persuasion |
| OBJ-02 proof / results / contract before I pay | T1 | PARTIAL → counted surviving | ownership + terms can be checked before and after paying (dissolves "is it real"), but "show me results" survives — no Readymerce review or result exists on file (PT-03, PT-07, PT-18) |
| OBJ-03 this is (probably) a scam / I've been scammed before | T1 | YES | a store in her name with payouts to her bank and a team still working after launch is the opposite of "took the money and left" |
| OBJ-04 $500 is only the start — hidden costs | T1 | NO | BD-1 says nothing about cost; answered by the published exclusions (PT-05) — the managed fee stays unpublished (PT-02) |
| OBJ-05 talk to spouse / call me back | T2 | NO | a HABIT force; untouched by any belief about the product |
| OBJ-06 I don't have the time | T2 | YES | the team works the store after launch (PT-04, PT-08) |
| OBJ-07 how fast / how much will it make — guaranteed? | T1 | NO | PT-09 / F-13: no income guarantee — survives by design; must be answered honestly |
| OBJ-08 DFY vendors don't deliver — I end up doing it myself | T2 (T1 in AV-02's view) | YES | "a team keeps working on it after launch" is the direct contrary |
| OBJ-09 I won't show my face / market myself | T3 | YES | ad testing and monitoring are in the package (F-05) |
| OBJ-10 stop calling me | T3 | NO | a channel objection (outbound calls), not a product belief |
| OBJ-11 won't risk my job / benefits / family money | T2 | NO | answered by the Honest Box (only money she can afford to lose) |
| OBJ-12.AV-02 the 7-day refund isn't realistic | T2 | NO | refund terms are a fact to state (PT-15), not a belief to shift |
| OBJ-13.AV-02 is this company even real | T1 | PARTIAL → counted surviving | ownership is checkable; legitimacy of the company itself is not (PT-16 ScamAdviser 0/100, ScamDoc 25%, F-16 no BBB profile) |
| OBJ-14.AV-02 will I lose access / is the store actually mine | T2 | YES | the store is in her name from day one (F-11) |
| OBJ-15.AV-02 what happens after launch — will they just leave | T1 | YES | the core of M-2 — but the post-launch window is unpublished (PT-08) → proof, not belief, carries the rest |

**Verdict: `DOMINO: PARTIAL`** — T1 surviving: OBJ-01, OBJ-02 (results half), OBJ-04, OBJ-07, OBJ-13 (company legitimacy half).
**Alternative framing (one sentence, for the surviving T1):** "What you pay for is a store in your name, a written list of what is and isn't included, and a team that stays after launch — all things you can check before and after you pay; what it will earn nobody can promise you."
**mechanism_note → Step 10 / 12:** LOCK-B dissolves the "took the money and left" fear but not the proof-before-money demand; for AV-02 the domino needs its transparency half (PT-04 includes / PT-05 excludes / PT-15 refund as conditioned) stated beside M-2, and the product needs (a) a published minimum post-launch window per tier (10 `product_would_need`) and (b) a first-100 review trail (P-12.AV-02) before OBJ-02/OBJ-13 can dissolve. OBJ-01 and OBJ-07 are qualification objections — the honest copy lets them filter out non-buyers (the operator's tire-kicker problem) instead of dissolving them.

`BD-1: T1 dissolved 3/8 (OBJ-03, OBJ-08 [T1 in AV-02 view], OBJ-15.AV-02; OBJ-02 + OBJ-13 half each, counted surviving), T2 dissolved 2/5 (OBJ-06, OBJ-14.AV-02), T3 1/2, verdict PARTIAL`

### §2.AV-03 — Stuck Starter (carried from 11-partials/11-BELIEFS.AV-03.md §2)

#### §2 · BIG DOMINO — BD-1.AV-03 (this avatar's candidate; Step 12 reconciles one per brand)  ⟨AV-03⟩

- **bd_statement:** "A store that is finished for you and kept working on after it goes live — in your own name — is the key to finally replacing your job with money coming in from something you own."
- **md_id:** MD-01 (leaderboard #1, 08 §12) · **lf8_root:** #1 (routes #1 > #3 > #5 > #8) · **desire_wording:** "I want to replace my job with money coming in from something I own" (DS-01, Q-O-0053 [R-OWNED]) — entered, for AV-03, through MD-04 "finally have the thing I started exist" (Q-O-0153 [R-OWNED] "I developed one, but I never launched it. That's the problem."), per 08's entry rule.
- **mechanism_ref:** M-2 = LOCK-B MK-06 UMS (post-launch review + corrections + rollout, CS-08..CS-10) resting on M-1 = LOCK-B UMP (the builder leaves on launch day). Not a feature claim: it names the new opportunity (a store that is not left alone), not a result.
- **domino test** (every OBJ in §8, "if he fully believed BD-1, is the objection irrelevant?"):

| OBJ-## | tier | dissolved? | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| OBJ-01.AV-03 "show me exactly what the $500 pays for" | T1 | NO | believing the after-launch work is the key sharpens the question ("is it in the $500?") — needs the published list (P-02, P-10), not a belief |
| OBJ-02.AV-03 "Is it something I have to run on my own?" | T1 | **YES** | the domino is the direct answer: a team keeps working after it goes live |
| OBJ-03.AV-03 "I'm not able to get the funds for it" | T1 | NO | a funds constraint; no belief removes it (Honest Box routes it) |
| OBJ-04.AV-03 "Just give me a big a figure" | T1 | NO | a mechanism cannot supply a number, and none may be given (PT-09, F-13) |
| OBJ-05.AV-03 "I won't be when I start spending money" (hidden costs) | T1 | NO | cost transparency (PT-05), not belief |
| OBJ-06.AV-03 "Have you tried to reach this number back" (is this real) | T1 | NO | legitimacy screen; PT-16 low automated trust scores sit against us |
| OBJ-07.AV-03 "when I'm more prepared" | T2 | **YES** | the preparing he waits for is the build and the after-launch work the domino hands over |
| OBJ-08.AV-03 "why would someone buy from a new shop" | T2 | NO | competition fear; answered by data-picked, staged products (LOCK-A), not by the handover domino |
| OBJ-09.AV-03 "Do I just choose what I want to sell" | T2 | **YES** | if the store keeps being worked on after launch (review, replace, roll in), the day-one pick stops being the whole bet |
| OBJ-10.AV-03 "how can i take back your money in seven" | T2 | NO | refund mechanics (PT-15) |
| OBJ-11.AV-03 "What would stop me from just doing this?" | T2 | **YES** | doing it himself also ends at launch day — the key is the part after |

`BD-1: T1 dissolved 1/6, T2 3/5, verdict DOMINO: PARTIAL` — surviving T1: OBJ-01, OBJ-03, OBJ-04, OBJ-05, OBJ-06 (all money / legitimacy / transparency screens voiced on owned calls).
- **alternative framing (one sentence):** "One published $500 price, every extra cost named up front, a store in your name, and a team that's still on it after it goes live — that's how the store you've been stuck on finally exists and starts earning."
- **mechanism_note → Step 10 / 12:** the LOCK-B domino dissolves the abandonment fear but not the owned-call money/legitimacy screen; for AV-03 the survivors must be carried by transparency proof (PT-01, PT-04, PT-05, PT-15, F-11) placed immediately after the mechanism, never by a bigger claim. Also: M-1 (MK-06) only PARTIALLY explains AV-03's core failed solution FS-05 (the stall is pre-launch) — see §9.

His local stage is a reset (0 ads reach him) while the pooled market is S4 — so this lane DIFFERS from the market lane (S4 mechanism+NI); but his rung is AW-4/5 and he has heard the enlarged claims first-hand (agency quotes, "fully automate" pitches), so the claim is carried by proof of the post-launch scope (07 §15 CP-04 recommended: PROOF of the management scope PT-08, Promise/Proof lead, static or ~10-second clip), and LOCK-B's stage4_axes "surer (someone reads the first data)" + "overcomes old limitation (build-and-leave)" become the comparison. New Information NI-04 (whose name is on the store) is a secondary beat only (ownership is F-11-proven, but it is AV-02's door, not his).
- `entry` — **MAY say:** his situation directly (he owns a business; he has stores sitting; he has priced agencies) · "we stay after it goes live" / "after launch day" (the Name-it pair) · the $500 first step and what it includes · "you approve the budgets, you see the reports" · his own word "automate" only inside his quote. **MAY NOT say:** "done-for-you" / "turnkey" as the hook word (0 of 307) · "fully automated" / "hands-free" as our claim (MC-02; 07 AW-4 must_not_be_told) · any income, ROI speed or success-rate figure (PT-09; DNS-01/02) · "we manage your store" (BURNED, DNS-05) · a story lead or a slow re-explanation of what a store build is (AW-4: too slow; EK-01) · "no sales"/"failed" — his stores SAT, he never said they failed.
- `starting_belief`: **B-01.AV-07** (live_wire NO) — "I had one before. I just ended it because I was dealing with a lot" (Q-O-0097) · "I'm a computer consultant and I have very little time." (Q-O-0154) · scene: "three or four Shopify and other stores that are just sitting there" (Q-O-0103).
- `route_architecture`: **comparison** (similar people, different outcomes) — 09 route_hint. Two owners, the same kind of store: one sits in the admin for years; the other is worked every week after it goes live. The variable is not the build, the product or the owner — it is whether anyone stays on the store after go-live.
- `micro_belief_sequence`: Belief 1 "You run a business; the store side stalled because nobody had the hours" `ACCEPTED ← Q-O-0097, Q-O-0154, Q-O-0103` → Belief 2 "Same store, same owner type: one sits for years, one gets worked every week after it goes live" `COMPARISON ← Q-O-0103 vs Q-E-0003 "they handle the backend" (competitor review — SIMILAR PRODUCT CONTEXT, used as language, never as Readymerce proof)` → Belief 3 "The difference isn't the build: the platform's own guide has builders hand the store over 'when it's ready to go live'" `CAUSAL ← P-06 Shopify.dev [R-PAGE via Exa]` → Belief 4 (B-02) "The weekly work after it goes live — reading first results, fixing listings, testing the next product — is what separates a store that runs from one that sits" `CAUSAL ← UNSOURCED (VOC only) — BIGGEST LEAP` → mechanism (LOCK-B M-1: the builder's job ends at go-live; M-2: a team stays on after) → B-03 "so it's not 'do it myself' or '$5k–$20k'" → **solution requirement** → product.
- `solution_requirement`: **"I need someone who stays on the store after it goes live and does the weekly work — at a price I can try before I commit to more — while I still approve the budgets and see what's happening."** (he can predict this before the name: Q-O-0029 "I'll start with the 500, and then I'll hopefully graduate" + Q-O-0001 "you guys … list the products … generate advertisements")
- `biggest_leap`: **BIGGEST LEAP: "the weeks after go-live decide whether a store runs or sits" is UNSOURCED (VOC-only — 10 R-3 B5) and Readymerce's post-launch scope is "agreed on the fit call" (PT-08), with no published minimum window and no client post-launch report on file (PT-07, PT-18).**
- `route_ref`: **BUILT-HERE** (comparison) — adapts 10 ROUTE 3 "The Weeks After Launch" B3–B5 (its Best Segment "I don't have time to build a store around my job" is CP-01, not the owner); 10 ROUTE 6 (the only 10 comparison route) is CP-06/Etsy-search and does not match his starting belief.

- **lane_applied:** **NEW INFORMATION first, carrying the New Mechanism DESCRIBED (not featured)** — reason: (1) his local stage is unscanned, so 10's rule for PB-06 applies ("LATE-BOUND → all three lanes"); (2) the only bodies aimed at CP-03 are 2 of 3 ENLARGED-CLAIM — the claim register he already distrusts (SK-02 "too many promises"; Q-Y-0016 "make it seem like easy money when it's really not"), so the S1–2 lane (claim + Name-it) would be disbelieved and the S4 lane ("made easier/surer" — an enlarged mechanism claim) would read as one more promise; (3) 07 §15 soph_gap_check CP-03 recommends NEW INFORMATION ("the untested first release", SR-02); (4) New Identity is unsupported for him (no S5 read; 10 §16b owner-not-operator identity is not his yet). **Differs from the market lane: YES** — DFY-only S2 → "claim first + Name-it pair"; pooled S4 → "mechanism made easier/quicker/surer + New Information"; AV-01 gets New Information → Described mechanism, no claim lead, mechanization intensity "Describe it" (10 LOCK-A default for PB-06).
- **entry (what the opener may / may not say at AW-2→3):** MAY — his loss in his own numbers and words ("still no sales", "zero sales … lost 254 dollars", "didn't sell one product"), "plenty of us losing", "it isn't easy money", the new information "sellers who find one tested five to ten". MAY NOT — "done for you"/"turnkey"/"we build your store" in the hook (0 of 307 passages; 07 AW-2 must_not_be_told), "winning product" as a promise, "easy", any income figure ("91%", "$10k a month" — DNS-01/02.AV-01), the product name before the solution requirement, anything that calls closing his store a mistake, any promise to fix his existing store (no PT supports a takeover — 07 §14a PB-06 Z).

- **route_architecture:** failed-solution (what he tried performed a different job: it tested one guess, it could not find a seller)
- **micro_belief_sequence:** Belief 1 (B-106.AV-01) Plenty of people lose money on a first store — I'm not the only one → Belief 2 (B-101.AV-01) Most of those stores tried one product, or one batch, and closed when it didn't sell → Belief 3 (B-105.AV-01) Fixing the store or buying more clicks for that same product didn't change it → Belief 4 (B-107.AV-01) Sellers who do find a product that sells usually tested five to ten first, each on a small budget → Belief 5 (B-102.AV-01 broken) So my store didn't show it can't work for me; it showed one guess missed → mechanism (M-1): the first store got one shot → **solution requirement** → product: Readymerce's staged release, each product tested on a budget he approves, an early review, what doesn't sell replaced (PT-04, F-05, F-07) — in a store in his name (F-11).
- **solution_requirement (he can predict it before the product name):** "The second try has to be several small tries, not one big one — products put up in turns, each on a budget I approve and can stop, with someone reading the results early and swapping what isn't selling, in a store that stays in my name."
- **biggest_leap:** BIGGEST LEAP: that Readymerce's $500 package actually runs enough stages and product tests to count as "several tries" — PT-19 per-stage product count UNKNOWN; PT-05 excludes ad spend, so the tries cost him extra ad money; 10 product_would_need (products per stage, default test budget + stop rule, who funds ad spend). Second, smaller leap: Belief 4 rests on a practitioner guide (Product Lair 2024), not a study.
- **route_ref:** R-1 "The Second Shot" (10 §13c; Best Segment CP-03 "I tried it, it didn't work, I lost money" — matches 09 route_hint `failed-solution`). Beat B3 (B-105.AV-01) is added from R-4 "600 Clicks, Zero Sales" so the SOLUTION-BREAK is broken before the reveal.

### §7.AV-07 — Owner Wants It Automated ★ (carried from 11-partials/11-BELIEFS.AV-07.md §7)

#### 7 · BELIEF SEQUENCE — BS-AV-07 (backward-chained, presented forward)  ⟨AV-07⟩
**Backward chain** (destination first): `final_buying_belief` = FB-AV-07 + BB-V "now": *"Someone who keeps working the store after it goes live is how my online side finally runs — and starting with the $500 step now costs less than another year of stores sitting there."* (Q-O-0029 "I'll start with the 500…" · Q-O-0054 "I just wanna go through with it" [R-OWNED]) ← just before it: **B-04** it is managed, not automatic — I approve budgets, I read reports ← **B-03** there is a third option between DIY and a $5k–$20k agency (THE ONE BELIEF) ← **B-06** it doesn't take a big ad budget — small capped tests ← **B-05** my store doesn't have to out-price the big stores ← **B-02** a built store isn't a running store; the weeks after go-live decide it ← **B-01** (held, live_wire NO) I couldn't keep it going next to my business = **THE OPENING**. No new bridge B-## was needed (all steps are ladder rows).

**Forward (plain paragraphs, research object — not approved copy):**
1. You already run a business. At some point you opened a store next to it — maybe three or four — and then the business needed you, and the stores sat. Nobody failed here; there were just no spare hours. *(B-01; Q-O-0097, Q-O-0154, Q-O-0103)*
2. Put two owners side by side with the same kind of store. One store sits in the admin for years. The other gets looked at every week after it goes live — first results read, listings fixed, the next product tested. The difference isn't the build. It's whether anyone stayed. The platform's own guide for store builders has them hand the store over "when it's ready to go live" — that's where a builder's job ends, and it's exactly where the weekly work starts. *(B-02; P-06, P-01)*
3. That work doesn't mean beating the big stores on price or buying a lot of ads. It means products picked from what people search for, released in stages, each with a small test at a budget you approve — and someone reading what the test shows. *(B-05, B-06; F-03, F-05, PT-04, PT-05)*
4. So the choice was never only "do it myself" or "$5,000 to $20,000 for an agency". There's a published first step: $500 for the build and research, a team that stays on after it goes live, and the ongoing level agreed — and priced — on the call before you commit to more. You approve the budgets; you see the reports; it's managed, not automatic. *(B-03, B-04; PT-01, PT-02, PT-04, PT-08)*
5. The stores that sat cost you years. The first step is $500, refundable within 7 days until you accept delivery or add a service. *(BB-V; P-03 as conditioned — PT-15)*

| WHERE HE IS (Q-##) | WHERE HE MUST ARRIVE (B-##) |
|---|---|

| "bigger stores are better priced … I simply can't compete" (Q-F-9008) | B-05: don't out-price them — pick from demand, test small |
| "we don't have the budget to be able to do a lot of ads" (Q-O-0005) · "it's slowed down so much" (Q-O-0124) | B-06: a few capped tests at a budget I approve |

### §7.AV-01 — Store That Died (carried from 11-partials/11-BELIEFS.AV-01.md §7)

#### 7 · BELIEF SEQUENCE — BS-AV-01 (backward chain, presented forward)  ⟨AV-01⟩

**Backward derivation (destination first):**
- `final_buying_belief` = FB-AV-01 + BB-V ([D] composed from Q-O-0109 "I'm looking for a steady income" and Q-Y-0027): *A store that gets several small, capped tries is how I finally get a steady income of my own — and I'd rather give it that second try now than let the first store be the last word.* ( BB-V lever from WHAT-MADE-THEM-ACT: Q-B-0004 "I was afraid to ever try again, but eventually I did", Q-Y-0015 "i failed the challenge … that's why i'm going to continue")
- ← just before: **B-104.AV-01** (BB-I, live wire honored) "I'm right to guard my money — this time I can see every cost, the store and payouts are mine, no test spends a dollar I didn't approve."
- ← before that: **B-103.AV-01** (BB-III, live wire honored) "It isn't easy money — and a store in my name that tests small first is a different setup from the one I was sold."
- ← before that: **solution requirement accepted** (BB-III) "the second try needs several small tries, read early."
- ← before that: **B-102.AV-01 broken** (BB-II, THE ONE BELIEF) "my stores failing doesn't show I can't do this; each got one guess."
- ← before that: **B-107.AV-01** (bridge, M-1) "a store finds what sells by testing several products small and early."
- ← before that: **B-105.AV-01** "fixing the store and buying clicks for the same product didn't change it."
- ← before that: **B-101.AV-01** "the product I tried didn't sell — and it was the only real try."
- ← **OPENING: B-106.AV-01** "plenty of us lose on a first store" — held (Q-Y-0027), live_wire NO → stop.

**Forward (5 plain paragraphs — the page and ad read this as order, not copy):**
1. There's plenty of us who put money into a first store and lost it — you're not the only one who watched the clicks come in and the orders never did. And no, it isn't easy money, whatever the videos said. (B-106.AV-01, B-103.AV-01 agreed)
2. Your product didn't sell. That part is true. What nobody pointed out is that it was the only real try your store got — one product, or one batch switched on at once, and ads until the money ran out. Fixing the store and buying more clicks for that same product was never going to change the answer. (B-101.AV-01, B-105.AV-01)
3. The sellers who do end up with a product that sells mostly didn't get it on the first try. The ones who write about it tested five, sometimes ten products, each on a small budget, and cut what didn't move. Some had nine failed stores before the one that paid for the rest. (B-107.AV-01, P-104.AV-01, P-106.AV-01)
4. So your store didn't prove this can't work for you. It proved one guess missed. What a second try needs isn't a better guess — it's several small tries, each on a budget you approve and can stop, with someone reading the results early and swapping what isn't selling, in a store that stays in your name. (B-102.AV-01 broken → solution requirement)
5. That's how a Readymerce store is launched — in stages, not all at once — and you see every cost before you pay: the $500 build, then the platform fees and the ad budget you set. No income is promised, and the refund terms are written out with their conditions. If you still want the thing you started, it's worth giving it more than one shot. (B-104.AV-01, BB-V; PT-04, PT-05, PT-09, PT-15, F-11)

### §7.AV-04 — Paycheck-Tethered (carried from 11-partials/11-BELIEFS.AV-04.md §7)

#### 7 · BELIEF SEQUENCE — BS-AV-04 (backward-chained, presented forward)  ⟨AV-04⟩

**Backward chain (destination → opening):**
- `final_buying_belief` = FB-AV-04 + BB-V now → "a store tested in small steps I approve, while I keep the job, is how I get a stable income flow — and I can start it now, without quitting." (B-42.AV-04 target)
- just before it: "a staged launch lets the store prove itself before anything bigger is risked" (B-45.AV-04 target) + "nothing to learn; my part is approving and reading" (B-46.AV-04 target)
- before that: "one small try only proves one guess missed; stores find what sells over several tries" (B-47.AV-04 target, the bridge — minted here)
- before that: "the time-hungry work is work a team can carry; it wasn't me, it was the hours" (B-43.AV-04 + B-44.AV-04 targets)
- before that — **the opening, a held belief (live_wire: NO):** "I work a full time job as well, so am only able to attend to my store after hours" (B-41.AV-04, Q-F-0015).

`belief_sequence["AV-04"] = [B-41.AV-04, B-44.AV-04, B-43.AV-04, B-47.AV-04, B-45.AV-04, B-46.AV-04, B-42.AV-04]`

**Forward (the order a page or ad reads it):**

1. You've got a job that pays. The store — if there is one — gets what's left: evenings, a Sunday. That's not a lack of drive. It's arithmetic. *(B-41.AV-04, Q-F-0015)*
2. And a new store isn't a weekend job. In its first weeks somebody has to pick which product goes live, set the ad, read what happened and cut what isn't moving — again and again. Nobody does that well in the hours after a shift. That's why so many after-hours stores get one try and then get closed. *(B-44.AV-04, B-43.AV-04)*
3. Here's the part nobody tells you: one try never proved much. People who end up with a product that sells usually tested five to ten first, each with a small budget. A store that got one guess only showed that one guess missed. *(B-47.AV-04, P-05)*
4. You already have the right rule: make sure it's working before you risk anything bigger. The only question is who runs the small tests that show it — because it can't be your evenings. *(B-45.AV-04)*
5. So the store has to run beside the job, not on top of it: products go live in stages, each tested on a budget you approve, with an early review that shows you what's selling and what gets replaced. You approve and you read; you don't get trained. The store stays in your name. *(B-46.AV-04, M-2, PT-04, F-11)*
6. You keep the salary. You don't quit anything. You just stop waiting for a "ready" that never comes — the first small test is how you find out. *(B-42.AV-04, BB-V)*

### §7.AV-02 — Paid-and-Got-Nothing (carried from 11-partials/11-BELIEFS.AV-02.md §7)

#### §7 · BELIEF SEQUENCE — BS-AV-02 (backward-chained, presented forward)  ⟨AV-02⟩

**Backward chain (destination first):**
- `final_buying_belief` = FB-AV-02 + BB-V: "If what burned me was a store that was never in my name and a builder who left once it went live, then a store opened in my name that a team keeps working on after launch is how I finally try again without being robbed — **and I can check it now, before I pay, instead of waiting for the next pitch to call me.**"
- just before it → **B-07.AV-02** (bridge, minted here): I can check this one myself — includes/excludes, refund conditions, the owner field in my own login.
- just before that → **B-04.AV-02**: paying someone to build it wasn't wrong; the version I bought stopped at go-live.
- just before that → **B-03.AV-02** (M-1): the store sat in the builder's account and the builder left the day it went live.
- just before that → **B-02.AV-02 broken** (THE ONE BELIEF): not every offer is the same — I already act as if some are real (I check).
- just before that → **B-01.AV-02** (live wire, honored): I was right to call what happened a scam.
- bottom = **opening**: B-03's held current — "they said they would build a store, but i am building the store???" (Q-F-0004), `live_wire: NO`. Steps: 6 (opening → B-01 → B-02 → B-03 → B-04 → B-07 → final).

**Forward, in plain paragraphs (research object — not approved copy; must pass the claims router):**

1. You paid someone to build you a store. What came back was half-done, or locked — "contact an administrator", when the administrator should have been you — or it was handed to you and left for you to finish.
2. You were right to call that what it was. You weren't paranoid then and you aren't now.
3. But notice something you still do. You check. You ask for the reviews, the contract, the proof it actually works. Nobody checks something they believe is always a lie — you check because you know some of these are real. What you've never been given is a way to tell them apart.
4. Here is the pattern in the stories that went wrong. The store was set up inside the builder's own account and was only "transferred" later — and until it is, the platform itself treats it as theirs. And the builder's job ended on the day the store went live, which is the day the real work starts: reading the first results, fixing what's off.
5. So the thing to check isn't the promise. It's two plain questions: whose name is the store in from the first day, and who is still working on it the week after it goes live? Paying someone to build it was never the mistake — buying a build that ended at go-live was.
6. You can check both yourself, before and after you pay: what the $500 includes and what it doesn't, when the refund closes, and — the day it opens — your own name as the owner in your own login.

| "they said they would build a store, but i am building the store???" (Q-F-0004) | B-04.AV-02 — the vehicle wasn't wrong; the version bought stopped at go-live |

| "go do a Google review of your company and see" (Q-O-0102) | B-07.AV-02 — she can check this one herself, before and after paying |

### §7.AV-03 — Stuck Starter (carried from 11-partials/11-BELIEFS.AV-03.md §7)

#### §7 · BELIEF SEQUENCE — BS-AV-03 (backward-chained, presented forward)  ⟨AV-03⟩

- **final_buying_belief** (= FB-AV-03 + BB-V "now"): "A store finished for me and not left alone after it goes live, in my name, is how I finally build an income of my own — and every extra week on the settings only keeps it from existing." (urgency: Q-Y-0053 "After weeks of overthinking, I finally committed … a huge weight off my shoulders"; negative mirror Q-O-0153 [R-OWNED] "I developed one, but I never launched it. That's the problem.")
- **backward chain:** final ← **B-04** (a service is the right vehicle only if its job includes the weeks after launch, it reports to me, the store stays mine) ← **B-03 target** (the first weeks after go-live decide the store; someone must still be on it) ← **S4 bridge** inside B-03 (builders' job ends at go-live — P-04, Q-F-0018, Q-B-8301) ← **B-03 current, "yes" half** + **B-06** (having it built is sensible; data, not my gut, picks the product) ← **B-02** (it isn't me — capable people stall here; live wire honored) ← **B-01** (setup is weeks of links that don't get me live) — **held, live_wire NO → THE OPENING**. B-05 (EXTERNAL) is answered off-chain (FAQ / objection OBJ-08) so the chain stays one line. Bridge steps minted: 0 new (every step is B-01..B-06).
- **forward (plain paragraphs, not final copy):**
  1. You opened the account and picked a theme. Then the weeks started — one link leads to ten more, and none of them gets the store live. (B-01 · Q-F-0006, Q-F-0013)
  2. That isn't a verdict on you. Capable people — people who ran real businesses — stall at exactly this step. The setup was never the part you needed to learn. (B-02 · Q-F-0012, Q-F-8302)
  3. So having someone build it makes sense. It does. But listen to the people who did: it went live, and then it went wrong — and the builder was already gone, because the builder's job ends the day the store goes live. (B-03 current → S4 · Q-F-0018, Q-B-8301, P-04)
  4. The weeks after launch are where a new store is made or lost — which products get a small test, what the first results say, what gets fixed or swapped. That is also where "what should I sell?" really gets answered: by testing, not by guessing right on day one. (B-03 target, B-06 · BIGGEST LEAP NB-3)
  5. So the store you need is one built in your name that someone keeps working on after it goes live — you see the reports and approve the budgets. That's how Readymerce is set up; and every week spent on the settings is another week the store doesn't exist. (B-04, BB-V · PT-04, F-11, Q-Y-0053)

- **honest_timeline (merged):** `first_sign`: procedural only — the store opened in the owner's name and configured, then a staged product release, a capped ad test at a budget the owner approves and an "early performance review" (PT-04, F-05, F-11); build "7 up to 10 days" is a sales-call statement ([R-OWNED] T-09; C-0022) + "within a matter of days" [R-SNIPPET] — **not a PDP fact, never printed as a promise until the operator publishes it**; the early-review day count: `NULL — searched: PT-04, PT-06, PT-08, F-05, F-07`. `full_effect`: `NULL — searched: PT-06, PT-09, PT-11..13` (no buyer-reported time-to-first-sale or profit; "Results are not typical and are not guaranteed"). `source`: PT-04, PT-06, PT-08, PT-09, [R-OWNED] T-09. All 5 partials agree; never an overnight transformation, never a salary date, never "profitable in a week".
- **honest_box (merged):**
  - `complaint` (top recurring first-use complaint — competitor reviews, SIMILAR PRODUCT CONTEXT, SAMPLE(10) 1★/negative rows): the store went live and then nobody helped and nothing sold / promises not kept — "I Never received any help to bring visitors to my store … 1 month and not one sale" (Q-E-0010) · "Not 1 item has been listed for sale in several months" (Q-E-0006) · "They promised the moon, but … they do not deliver" (Q-E-0008) · market: "When the store was transferred to me, the store was shutdown almost immediately" (Q-F-0018) · owned: "with all of their management and experience my store didn't even make $20" (Q-O-0101 [R-OWNED]); category-typical PT-10 (R-16/R-17 "doesn't eliminate the need to attract customers").
  - `limitation` (the site's own, union): no income, sales or profit guarantee (PT-09, F-13) · product costs, platform fees and ad spend are NOT in the $500 (PT-05) · the ongoing-management level is agreed on the fit call, no published minimum window (PT-08, PT-02) · products per stage and the test budget unpublished (PT-19) · the 7-day refund covers the $500 build fee only and voids on accepting delivery, any add-on, or a further-work request (PT-15) — while the build takes 7–10 days · no PT-## says Readymerce takes over an existing store (07 SR-04) · account approval / payout holds on Shopify/Etsy not guaranteed (risk disclaimer; MK-13) · the site is new and scores low on automated trust tools (PT-16) · portal / account manager UNKNOWN (PT-19).
  - `not_for` (merged, one line): **"Best for someone who wants a store in their own name that a team keeps working on after it goes live — tested in small steps on a budget they approve — and who can fund platform fees and a small ad-test budget on top of the $500. If you need guaranteed income or sales, a store that runs itself with no budget or decisions from you, your salary replaced by a set date, your existing store taken over as-is, or you'd be using money you can't afford to lose — this may not be the right fit."** (union of not_for.AV-07/01/04/02/03)
  - `remedy_line` (merged): "Before you pay, see every cost on one page — the $500 build, the platform fee, the ad budget you set and the quoted management level — and ask on the fit call, in writing, how many products go live per stage, the test budget and stop rule, the date of the first review and how long the team stays after launch."

If what you need is guaranteed sales or a set income — or you can't fund platform fees, products and an ad-test budget on top of the $500 — this may not be the right fit."
  - `remedy_line`: "Before you pay, ask on the fit call for the post-launch window and the first test budget in writing — and know the refund closes 7 days after payment, or sooner if you approve delivery or add a service."
